thanks to Proposition A1,
since
\Lambda^{2}>a+|\Lambda|\beta
holds for

\Lambda>\frac{\beta}{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\beta^{2}}{4}+a}
.
\square

Therefore,
considering the problem
(IVP)
:

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}{u}}{dt^{2}}+{B}\frac{d{u}}{dt}+{L}{u}={0},

\displaystyle{u}(t)\in\mathsf{D}({L})\quad\mbox{for}\quad\forall t\geq 0,

\displaystyle{u}=\overset{\circ}{{u}},\quad\frac{d{u}}{dt}=\overset{\circ}{{v}}\quad\mbox{at}\quad t=0,

we can claim

Theorem A

Suppose
\overset{\circ}{{u}}\in\mathsf{D}({L})
,

\overset{\circ}{{v}}\in\mathsf{Y}
and
f\in C([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{X})
.
Then the initial-boundary value problem
(IVP)
admits a unique solution

{u}\in C^{2}([0,+\infty[,\mathsf{X})\cap C^{1}([0,+\infty[,\mathsf{Y})\cap C([0,+\infty[,\mathsf{D}({L}))

and the energy

E(t)=\|{u}\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\|\dot{{u}}\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

enjoys the estimate

\sqrt{E(t)}\leq e^{\Lambda t}\Big(\sqrt{E(0)}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\Lambda s}\|f(s)\|_{\mathsf{X}}ds\Big),

where
\Lambda=1+a+\beta
.

Here
\mathsf{D}({L})
is equipped with the norm

(\|{u}\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\|{L}{u}\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2})^{1/2}
.

Appendix B

Let us imagine the motion
\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{p}(t)
of an infenitesimally small percel in the background
(\rho_{b},S_{b})
. The density
\rho_{p}(\bm{x}(t))
, pressure
P_{p}(\bm{x}(t))
, entropy density
S_{p}(\bm{x}(t))
of the percel obey the same EOS:
P=\mathscr{P}(\rho,S)
as the background.
Suppose that
P_{p}(\bm{x}(t))=P_{b}(\bm{x}(t))
.

Here we note the following:

Let
\rho=\mathscr{R}(\rho,S)
be the inverse function of
P=\mathscr{P}(\rho,S)
, so

\rho=\mathscr{R}(\mathscr{P}(\rho,S),S);

then it holds

\displaystyle\partial_{S}\mathscr{R}(P,S)

\displaystyle=-(\partial_{\rho}\mathscr{R}(P,S))(\partial_{S}\mathscr{P}(\mathscr{R}(P,S),S))

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial_{S}\mathscr{P}(\rho,S)}{\partial_{\rho}\mathscr{P}(\rho,S)}\Big|_{\rho=\mathscr{R}(P,S)};

therefore, by Definition
3
, (
2.10b
), we have

\partial_{S}\mathscr{R}(P,S)\nabla S=\rho\mathfrak{a}.

Now the equation of motion of the parcel is

\frac{d^{2}\bm{x}_{p}}{dt^{2}}=-\frac{\nabla P_{b}}{\rho_{p}}+\bm{F}(t,\bm{x}_{p}(t)),

while the background satisfies

0=-\frac{\nabla P_{b}}{\rho_{b}}+\bm{F}(t,\bm{x})

for some vector field of force.
( In our situation, we are concerned with

\bm{F}=-\nabla\Phi_{b}-(D\bm{v}_{b})\bm{v}_{b}-2\bm{\Omega}\times\bm{v}_{b}-\bm{\Omega}\times(\bm{\Omega}\times\bm{x}).

But we need not the specification of
\bm{F}
, wich contains both real and virtual forces.) Therefore the equation of motion is

\frac{d^{2}\bm{x}_{p}}{dt^{2}}=\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}-\frac{1}{\rho_{p}}\Big)\nabla P_{b}(\bm{x}_{p}(t)).

On the other hand we have approximately

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}-\frac{1}{\rho_{p}}

\displaystyle\approxeq\frac{1}{\rho_{b}^{2}}\partial_{S}\mathscr{R}(P_{b},S_{b})(\nabla S_{b}|\bm{x}_{p}(t)-\bm{x}_{p}(0))

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}(\mathfrak{a}_{b}|\bm{x}_{p}(t)-\bm{x}_{p}(0)).

Since
\nabla P_{b}
is parallel to
\bm{n}\approx\bm{n}_{0}=\bm{n}(\bm{x}_{p}(0))
,
we suppose that

\bm{x}_{p}(t)=X(t)\bm{n}_{0}.

Then

\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}-\frac{1}{\rho_{p}}\approxeq\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}\mathscr{A}_{b}\Big|_{\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{p}(0)}(X(t)-X(0),

and the equation of motion is

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}X}{dt^{2}}

\displaystyle\approxeq\frac{1}{\rho_{b}}\mathscr{A}_{b}(\nabla P_{b}|\bm{n}_{0})(X(t)-X(0))

\displaystyle\approxeq-\mathscr{N}_{b}^{2}\Big|_{\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{p}(0)}(X(t)-X(0)).

The solution of this equation is

X(t)=X(0)+C_{+}e^{\mathrm{i}Nt}+C_{-}e^{-\mathrm{i}Nt}\quad\mbox{where}\quad N:=\mathscr{N}_{b}(\bm{x}_{p}(0)).

Clearly

|X(t)-X(0)|=O(1)
for non-trivial
X(t)
if and only if
N\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore the definition (
2.15
) is justifiable.

Appendix C

We consider the initial value problem
(IV)
:

\frac{d^{2}u}{dt^{2}}+B\frac{du}{dt}+Lu=f(t),\quad u|_{t=0}=\overset{\circ}{u},\quad\frac{du}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}=\overset{\circ}{v}.

The unknown
u(t)
is valued in a Hilbert space
\mathsf{X}
.
\mathsf{Y}
is a Hilbert space which is densely imbedded in
\mathsf{X}
.
L
is a closed linear operator whose domain
\mathsf{D}(L)
is a dense subset of
\mathsf{Y}
.
It holds that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathsf{Y}}\geq\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}\quad\mbox{for}\quad\forall u\in\mathsf{Y}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[((L+a+1)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]=\|u\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}\quad\forall u\in\mathsf{D}(L),

where
a
is a non-negative number.
Noreover we suppose

|(Lu_{1}|u_{2})_{\mathsf{X}}-(u_{1}|Lu_{2})_{\mathsf{X}}|\leq 2\nu\|u_{1}\|_{\mathsf{X}}\|u_{2}\|_{\mathsf{X}}\quad\mbox{for}\quad\forall u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathsf{D}(L),

where
\nu
is a non-negative number.

B
is a bounded linear operator defined on

\mathsf{X}
into
\mathsf{X}
with

\beta:=\sup\frac{|\mathfrak{Re}[(Bv|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]|}{\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}}.

Remark
:
If
L
is symmetric, we take
\nu=0
. If
B
is skew-symmetric, then
\beta=0
.

We claim

Theorem C

If
\overset{\circ}{u}\in\mathsf{D}(L),\overset{\circ}{v}\in\mathsf{Y}

and
f\in C([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{X})
, then the problem
(IVP)
admits a unique solution

u\in C^{2}([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{X})\cap C^{1}([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{Y})\cap C([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{D}(L))
. The solution enjoys

\sqrt{E(t)}\leq e^{\kappa t}\sqrt{E(0)}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\kappa(t-s)}\|f(s)\|_{\mathsf{X}}ds.

Here

\kappa=\kappa(a,\nu,\beta):=\Big(\frac{5}{4}+\frac{a+\nu}{2}\Big)\vee\Big(1+\frac{a+\nu}{2}+\beta\Big).

and

E(t):=\|u(t)\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\Big\|\frac{du}{dt}\Big\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}.

Let us sketch the proof of
Theorem C
. First we claim

Proposition C1

Let
c\in\mathbb{R},\lambda>a+|c|\beta
. Then
\lambda+cB+L
admits the bounded inverse

(\lambda+cB+L)^{-1}
with

|\|(\lambda+cB+L)^{-1}\||_{\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda-a-|c|\beta}.

[formula]

Proof. Given
f\in\mathsf{X}
, we have to solve the equation

\lambda u+cBu+Lu=f.

[formula]

If
u\in\mathsf{D}(L)
satisfies (C2), then we have

\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda-a-|c|\beta}\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}.

In fact, (C2) implies

\mathfrak{Re}[(\lambda u+Lu|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+c\mathfrak{Re}[(Bu|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]=\mathfrak{Re}[(f|u)_{\mathsf{X}}],

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[(\lambda u+Lu|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{Re}[(a+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+(\lambda-a)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(\lambda-a)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2},

\displaystyle c\mathfrak{Re}[(Bu|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]

\displaystyle\geq-|c|\beta\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[(f|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}},

therefore

(\lambda-a-|c|\beta)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}\leq\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}.

Hence the range
\mathsf{R}(\lambda+cB+L)=\mathsf{D}((\lambda+cB+L)^{-1})
is a closed subspace of
\mathsf{X}
, since
\lambda+cB+L
is a closed operator. We claim that

\mathsf{R}(\lambda+cB+L)=\mathsf{X}
. In fact, consider
h\in\mathsf{X}
which satisfies

((\lambda+cB+L)u|h)_{\mathsf{X}}=0\quad\mbox{for}\quad\forall u\in\mathsf{D}(L).

Then
h\in\mathsf{D}(L^{*})
and

(u|(\lambda+cB^{*}+L^{*})h)_{\mathsf{X}}=0\quad\mbox{for}\quad\forall u\in\mathsf{D}(L).

Since
\mathsf{D}(L)
is dense in
\mathsf{X}
, we have

(\lambda+cB^{*}+L^{*})h=0,

and

\mathfrak{Re}[(\lambda+L^{*})h|h)_{\mathsf{X}}]+c\mathfrak{Re}[(B*h|h)_{\mathsf{X}}]=0.

Since

((\lambda+L^{*})h|h)_{\mathsf{X}}=((\lambda+L)h|h)_{\mathsf{X}}^{*},\quad(B^{*}h|h)_{\mathsf{X}}=(Bh|h)_{\mathsf{X}}^{*},

we have

(\lambda-a)\|h\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}-|c|\beta\|h\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\leq 0.

Therefore
h=0
, for
\lambda-a-|c|\beta>0
. Therefore

\mathsf{R}(\lambda+cB+L)=\mathsf{X}
and
(\lambda+cB+L)^{-1}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})
and enjoys (C1).
\square

Now we write the problem
(IVP)
as
(IVP‡)
:

\frac{dU}{dt}=\mathfrak{A}U+F,\quad U(0)=U_{0},

where

\displaystyle U=\begin{bmatrix}u\\
\\
v\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}u\\
\\
\dot{u}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}u\\
\\
\frac{du}{dt}\end{bmatrix},\quad U_{0}=\begin{bmatrix}\overset{\circ}{u}\\
\\
\overset{\circ}{v}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}=\begin{bmatrix}O&I\\
\\
-L&-B\end{bmatrix},\quad F=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\\
f\end{bmatrix}

and apply Hile-Yosida theory. We consider the problem in the Hilbert space

\mathsf{Z}=\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{X}
endowed with the norm

\|U\|_{\mathsf{Z}}=\Big[\|u\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\|\dot{u}\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

while

\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{A})=\mathsf{D}(L)\times\mathsf{Y}
.
We claim

Proposition C2
:
Put
\kappa=\kappa(a,\nu,\beta)
. Consider
n\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|n|
is large.

The equation

\Big(I-\frac{1}{n}\mathfrak{A}\Big)U=F,

F=(g,f)^{\top}\in\mathsf{Z}
given, admits a unique solution
U\in\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{A})
, and

\|U\|_{\mathsf{Z}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}}\|F\|_{\mathsf{Z}}.

That is,

\Big|\Big\|\Big(I-\frac{1}{n}\mathfrak{A}\Big)^{-1}\Big\|\Big|_{\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{Z})}\leq\frac{1}{1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}}.

Proof. We have to solve

\displaystyle u-\frac{1}{n}v=g(\in\mathsf{Y}),

\displaystyle v+\frac{1}{n}Bv+\frac{1}{n}Lu=f(\in\mathsf{X}).

Since that
|n|
is so large that

n^{2}>a+|n|\beta
. Then
Proposition C1
gurantees

(n^{2}+nB+L)^{-1}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})
and

|\|(n^{2}+nB+L)^{-1}\||_{\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{X})}\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}-a-|n|\beta}.

Keeping in mind this, we can find the solution

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=(n^{2}+nB+L)^{-1}(nBg+n^{2}g+nf),

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=n(u-g)=n\Big[(n^{2}+nB+L)^{-1}(nBg+n^{2}g+nf)-g\Big].

Note that
u\in\mathsf{D}(L)
but
v\in\mathsf{Y}
. Temporally we suppose that

g\in\mathsf{D}(L)
so that
v\in\mathsf{D}(L)
. In this situation, we are going to estimate

\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}
by

\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}
, where we put

\lambda:=1+a.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}=\|u\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}=\|g\|_{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}.

Using

g=u-\frac{1}{n}v,\quad f=v+\frac{1}{n}Lu+\frac{1}{n}Bv,

we see

\displaystyle((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}

\displaystyle=((\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}-\frac{\lambda}{n}((u|v)_{\mathsf{X}}+(v|u)_{\mathsf{X}})+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\Big((Lu|v)_{\mathsf{X}}+(Lv|u)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}((\lambda+L)v|v)_{\mathsf{X}},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\Big((v|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}+(Lu|v)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|Lu\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}((v|Bv)_{\mathsf{X}}+(Bv|v)_{\mathsf{X}})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}(Bv|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|Bv\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle=((\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|Lu\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}((\lambda+L)v|v)_{\mathsf{X}}+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\mathfrak{r},

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{2\lambda}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(u|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\frac{1}{n}\Big(-(Lv|u)_{\mathsf{X}}+(v|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big)+

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(Bv|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\frac{1}{n^{2}}(Bv|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\|Bv\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\lambda}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(u|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\frac{1}{n}\Big(-(Lv|u)_{\mathsf{X}}+(v|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big)+

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(Bv|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\Big(f-v-\frac{1}{n}Lu\Big|u\Big)_{\mathsf{X}}+\Big\|f-v-\frac{1}{n}Lu\Big\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}.

Since

\displaystyle\Big|-\frac{2\lambda}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(u|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]\Big|\leq\frac{\lambda}{|n|}(\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{n}\mathfrak{Re}\Big[-(Lv|u)_{\mathsf{X}}+(v|Lu)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big]\Big|\leq\frac{\nu}{|n|}(\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{2}{n}\mathfrak{Re}[(Bv|v)_{\mathsf{X}}]\Big|\leq\frac{2\beta}{|n|}\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{n}\mathfrak{Re}\Big[\Big(f-v-\frac{1}{n}Lu\Big|u\Big)_{\mathsf{X}}\Big]\Big|\leq\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2|n|}\|Lu\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\Big),

we have the estimtae

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[\mathfrak{r}]

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+2\beta+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\|Lu\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2|n|}\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}.

Consequently we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mathfrak{Re}[(\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\|u\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}+\Big(1-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+2\beta+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2|n|}\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2},

or

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\Big(\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\Big)\mathfrak{Re}[(\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+

\displaystyle+\Big(1-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+2\beta+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}.

If we take
\kappa>\kappa(\nu,a,\beta)=\Big(\frac{5}{4}+\frac{a+\nu}{2}\Big)\vee\Big(1+\frac{a+\nu}{2}+\beta\Big)
, it holds

\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)g|g)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|f\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\geq\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}\Big)^{2}\Big(\mathfrak{Re}[((\lambda+L)u|u)_{\mathsf{X}}]+\|v\|_{\mathsf{X}}^{2}\Big)

for
|n|\geq N_{*}
,
N_{*}
being a sufficiently large positive number.
In fact, we see

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)^{-1}\Big(1-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}\Big)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\kappa-\Big(\frac{5}{2}+a+\nu\Big)\Big)\frac{1}{|n|}+O\Big(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{2|n|}\Big)^{-1}\Big(1-\frac{1}{|n|}\Big(\lambda+\nu+2\beta+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}\Big)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\Big(2\kappa-\Big(2+a+\nu+2\beta\Big)\Big)\frac{1}{|n|}+O\Big(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\Big).

This means

\|F\|_{\mathsf{Z}}\geq\Big(1-\frac{\kappa}{|n|}\Big)\|U\|_{\mathsf{Z}}.

Passing to the limit, we can claim this is valid for
f\in\mathsf{Y},v\in Y_{0}
, and
\kappa=\kappa(\nu,a,\beta)

\square
.

Consequently we can apply
[
23
, Theorem and Corollary 1 of IX.9]

to conclude that

\mathfrak{A}
is the infenitesimal generator of an equi-continuous group of class (
C_{0}
)
of operators
\mathscr{S}_{t}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{Z}),-\infty<t<+\infty
; If
U_{0}\in\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{A})
and

F\in C([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{Z})
, then

U(t)=\mathscr{S}_{t}U_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{S}_{t-s}F(s)ds

is the solution of
(IVP‡)

of the class
C^{1}([0,+\infty[,\mathsf{Z})\cap C([0,+\infty[;\mathsf{D}(\mathfrak{A}))
. It enjoys

\|U(t)\|_{\mathsf{Z}}\leq e^{\kappa t}\|U_{0}\|_{\mathsf{Z}}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\kappa(t-s)}\|F(s)\|_{\mathsf{Z}}ds

for
t\geq 0
, since
|\|\mathscr{S}_{t}\||_{\mathcal{B}(\mathsf{Z})}\leq e^{\kappa t}
for
t\geq 0
.

Interpreting this conclusion to the words of
u
, we completes the proof of
Theorem C
.

Appendix D

Let us consider a motion of infinitesimally small parcel of fluid in the barotropic background
(\rho_{b},S_{b},\bm{v}_{b})
described in the non-rotating cylindrical coordinate system. So,

\bm{v}_{b}^{<}=\begin{bmatrix}v^{\varpi}\\
\\
v^{z}\\
\\
v^{\phi}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\\
0\\
\\
\varpi^{2}\Omega_{b}(\varpi)\end{bmatrix}

and the motion of the parcell is

\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{p}(t)=\begin{bmatrix}x_{p}^{1}(t)\\
\\
x_{p}^{2}(t)\\
\\
x_{p}^{3}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\varpi_{p}(t)\\
\\
z_{p}(t)\\
\\
\phi_{p}(t)\end{bmatrix}^{>}.

We suppose that
\displaystyle v_{p}^{\phi}(\bm{x}_{p}(0))=\frac{d}{dt}\phi_{p}(t)\Big|_{t=0}=0
.

The equation of motion of the paercell is

\frac{d^{2}\varpi_{p}}{dt^{2}}=K^{\varpi},

where
K^{\varpi}
is the
\varpi
-component of the force per unit mass, while the background satisfies

-\varpi\Omega_{b}(\varpi)^{2}=K^{\varpi}.

Here

K^{\varpi}=-\Big[\frac{1}{\rho}\frac{\partial P}{\partial\varpi}+\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial\varpi}\Big]_{b}.

Recall that the
\varpi
-component of the equation of motion reads

\Big[\frac{\partial}{\partial t}+v^{\varpi}\frac{\partial}{\partial\varpi}+v^{z}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}+\frac{v^{\phi}}{\varpi}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}\Big]v^{\varpi}-\frac{(v^{\phi})^{2}}{\varpi}+\frac{1}{\rho}\frac{\partial P}{\partial\varpi}+\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial\varpi}=0.

We suppose that the specific angular momentum

J_{p}=\varpi_{p}v_{p}^{\phi}
of the parcel coincides with that
J_{b}=\varpi^{3}\Omega_{b}(\varpi)
of the background. Then it holds that

v_{p}^{\phi}=\varpi_{p}^{2}\Omega_{b}(\varpi_{p})
.

Under these assumptions, the equation of motion of the parcel turns out to be

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}\varpi_{p}}{dt^{2}}

\displaystyle=K^{\varpi}=-\varpi\Omega_{b}^{2}=-\varpi\Big(\frac{v_{p}^{\phi}}{\varpi^{2}}\Big)^{2}=-\frac{1}{\varpi^{3}}(v_{p}^{\phi})^{2}

\displaystyle\approxeq-\frac{1}{\varpi^{3}}\Big[(v_{p}^{\phi})^{2}|_{t=0}+\frac{\partial}{\partial\varpi}(v_{p}^{\phi})^{2}\Big|_{t=0}(\varpi_{p}(t)-\varpi_{p}(0))\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\varpi^{3}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\varpi}(\varpi^{4}\Omega_{b}^{2})\Big|_{t=0}(\varpi_{p}(t)-\varpi(0)),

that is, approximately,

\frac{d^{2}\varpi_{p}}{dt^{2}}+\mathscr{C}(\varpi_{p}(0))(\varpi_{p}(t)-\varpi_{p}(0))=0.

Now it is clear that the motion
\varpi_{p}(t)
is bounded for any initial data if and only if

\mathscr{C}(\varpi_{p}(0))\geq 0
, the Rayleigh’s criterion.
